# Rules of Conduct for Public Comment at Council Meetings*

Members of the public may speak at a Council meeting upon recognition by the Mayor. Public comment may only occur during periods designated on the agenda for public comment or a public hearing. A person may speak for up to two (2) minutes during each of the two public comment periods on agenda items or non-agenda items. Walvers of the time requirement may only be granted in the discretion of the Mayor, and waivers to speak at a time other than a designated comment period may only be granted by the Council. Any person wishing to make a presentation longer than two minutes or requiring audio-visual equipment is asked to contact the City Clerk requesting to appear on a future agenda.

A person may only address Council from the podium. Only one person may occupy the podium at a time. All remarks are to be directed to the Mayor and Council. Speakers are not to engage in direct dialog with other meeting attendees.

Any person who violates the Rules of Conduct, disturbs the peace at the meeting, and/or interferes with the meeting may be warned, ordered to be seated, removed, and/or ticketed.

 $ ^{*} $This summarizes Council Resolution 04-18. Complete Rules, including guidelines for considering waivers, are available in the Council Chambers and from the City Clerk.

DISCUSSION - Downtown

DDA Director Mack stated the Farmers Market is going well, even though there were some cancellations due to weather, and even if they cancel, their spots are spoken for. The vendors said the holiday weekend went well. He then stated the DDA Board will be meeting next week and the results of the Transformation Strategy session will be presented by the facilitator, and it will then be shared with City Council after the board approves it. He further stated the DDA Board will be working on their mission/vision statement for Oakland County Main Street program. He further stated they are planning the Holiday Spectacular and they are looking at having a carriage ride there. Councilmember Bogert asked about the controversy about the South Lyon Pride Event. Mr. Mack stated he posted it on the web page, and some of the comments became counterproductive, and people were swearing, so he took the comments down. Councilmember Mosier asked about the process for the vendors such as the Twisted Cork and the Witches Hat. Mr. Mack stated that is a different process, it is for  $ \frac{1}{2} $ a season vendor and it offers 5 days of liquor license and Diana gives them a spot and their location would remain their spot.

they wouldn't be moved to another. Councilmember Mosier asked if he could update the sponsors on the DDA website.

## FIRE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Thorington stated there have been 587 instances to date. He said they have 3 openings in the Fire Department. He further stated they are waiting on parts for Engine 7-1.

## POLICE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Baaki stated they will be ordering the new car computers that are required for the new software updates implemented by CLEMIS. He stated they have ordered their drone and are preparing officers for training on its use, and it's a very capable drone. He further stated Lisa Werner left us and they are advertising to hire a new Administrative Assistant. He then stated that pending background checks, they will be hiring 2 new cadets. Councilmember Dilg asked about the drone. Chief Baaki stated the drone can help look for people. It is infrared, it has spot lights and he is impressed. Councilmember Dilg then asked about the license plate readers that Lyon Township have. Chief Baaki stated he can contact them; he thinks the cameras cost $3,000 per camera per year.

City Manager Zelenak stated as part of our efforts to make further improvements to our city road system, we went out for bid for crack sealing segments of our local roads. This contract is to conduct overband crank fill and asphalt mastic repair. He further stated we received 2 bids.

## MANAGER'S REPORT

City Manager Zelenak stated the developer for the Washington Manor project is still working through issues with MSHDA. He then stated Priority Waste has started their contract for garbage and recycling in the city this past Monday. He then stated 25% of the trucks are already getting fit for cameras to be installed. He then stated we still have concert dates available for Councilmembers to do the intro for the concerts if anyone is interested. He stated there will be 5 food trucks at this week's Concert in the Park, which is the Phoenix Theory Band with special guest Leah Brooke. He updated Council on the road

<a id="p5"></a>
*[Page 5 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=5)* (read by OCR; check the original)

project stating they have removed the curb and sidewalk in the Eagle Pointe Subdivision and finishing up pouring the new concrete. He then stated they expect to begin milling the asphalt this week. He then stated he recently toured pollinator gardens in Plymouth and Canton, and he is working with the groups that did the plantings to ensure that when we plant our pollinator garden that it will have a better chance of success. Further discussion was held regarding the pollinator gardens.

## COUNCIL COMMENTS

Councilmember Kurtzweil thanked the Cultural Arts Commission for putting together the tribute to Dayna Johnson on the boards throughout the city. She then stated everyone should take some time and visit this exhibit of her life. And a PDF of the pictures will be delivered to Craig and Doug Bakki. Again, thank you to the Cultural Arts Commission for this project of tribute. She then reminded everyone to attend our concerts in the park, they are great events and she encourages all her colleagues on Council to host one of them.

Councilmember Dilg stated she agrees with Councilmember Kurtzweil, she also has enjoyed seeing the images of Dayna Johnston downtown and she thanked the Cultural Arts Commission for honoring her. She then stated it looks like a great weekend ahead with the concert in the park, the Farmers Market and the Pride in the Park event.

Councilmember Bogert reminded everyone that South Lyon Pride event is this Sunday between 12-4pm and the young entrepreneur event is Saturday between 10-3pm.

Councilmember Hansen stated the clover is coming up very nicely in his backyard.

Councilmember Kennedy reminded everyone about this Friday's Concert in the Park that will be held in McHattie Park on the ball fields. It starts at 6pm with our guest performer Leah Brooke and then followed by the Phoenix Theory, a great band that has previously performed at the Christmas in July event last year at Heinanen Engineering. There will be several food trucks at the event so you can grab something to eat while you enjoy the music. And remember we're also having a food drive for Active Faith, so please bring along some non-perishable food items to donate. He then stated on Friday, July 19th there will be the first Movie in the Park in McHattie Park. There will be bounce houses, games and crafts for the kids and Snowy Owl will be there with their shaved ice delicacies. The movie will be the Lorax. Pre-movie entertainment starts at 6pm and the movie begins at dusk. He then stated he wanted to recognize and thank the McCauley Family for taking the time and effort to perform some repairs and repaint our Little Free Library in McHattie Park. The little Free Library was originally built in August of 2016 by the Friends of the Salem-South Lyon District Library and has been providing a variety of free reading material for young and old alike since then. The Friends of the Salem-South Lyon District Library include about two dozen volunteers and stewards who are part of an extraordinary effort to keep that library maintained and stocked. Last week, one family of those volunteers, the McCauleys, retiled the roof and repainted our Free Little Library. He then thanked Marcus, Emmy, Clara and Nora McCauley. And thank you also to all of the volunteers who pitch in to help take care of this jewel in our community throughout the year.

# AGENDA NOTE Unfinished Business Item #1

MEETING DATE: July 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Recommendation from Planning Commission on November 16, 2023

AGENDA TOPIC: Second reading of Ordinance to Amend the Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon by conditionally rezoning portions of the total 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, zoned I-1, Light Industrial, as follows: 24.46+/- acres of RM-3, Multiple-Family Residential and 1.63+/- acres R-3, Single-Family Residential. The remaining 8.08+/- acres will remain as I-1, Light Industrial.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Elevate Land Holdings, LLC has petitioned the City and requested a Conditional Rezoning of a portion of a 34.17+/- acre parcel on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, parcel #21-30-126-003, to allow 252 new multiple-family residential units, 3 single-family residential lots, and the remaining portion of the parcel to be developed with approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial development. The petitioner's request is pursuant to MCL 125.3405, which authorizes rezoning with conditions voluntarily offered by the owner of the property.

At its May 13 $ ^{th} $ 2024 meeting, the City Council reviewed a revised conditional rezoning request for the project, including a modified design of the entrance drive off of Ten Mile using an arched bridge design and other modifications to reduce impacts to the wetlands. The City Council approved the first reading of the ordinance and the preliminary site plan.

Following the May 13 $ ^{th} $ meeting and in response to comments received at the meeting, the applicant approached the City with an updated preliminary site plan that shifts the pedestrian pathway to run along the wetlands on the east side of the property and along Elk Lake Court to Dixboro Road. The updated plan provides a more clearly designated pedestrian pathway, a more scenic route for pedestrians, and maintains the 25-foot wetland setback by proposing the walkway outside of the setback area.

In order for the pathway to stay out of the wetland setback area, some plan elements have shifted slightly including a shift in the orientation of Buildings 14, 15, and 16; shift of the dog park outside of the wetland setback completely; shift of some parking areas and elimination of four parking spaces in the residential area and eight parking spaces in the industrial area; shift in garages and dumpster to the west; and shift in phase line (no impact on development).

These revisions required minimal changes to the legal descriptions of the rezoning areas, including an increase of the RM-3 area from 24.40 acres to 24.46 acres (+0.06 acres) and

<a id="p8"></a>
*[Page 8 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=8)* (read by OCR; check the original)

a decrease of the I-1 area from 8.14 acres to 8.08 acres (-0.06 acres). This represents an overall change of 0.2% of the area and has been determined to be a de minimis change.

THE CITY OF SOUTH LYON ORDAINS:

PART I. Amendment of Official Zoning Map. The Official Zoning Map of the City of South Lyon incorporated into the South Lyon Zoning Ordinance by Section 102-182 is hereby amended to conditionally rezone a portion of the vacant Property located on the east side of Dixboro Road, south of Ten Mile/Lake Street, Tax ID No. 21-30-126-003 and more fully described as:

COMMENCING AT THE SOUTHWEST CORNER OF SECTION 19, ALSO BEING THE NORTHWEST CORNER OF SECTION 30, T1N-R7E, LYON TOWNSHIP OAKLAND COUNTY, MICHIGAN; THENCE N03°22'30"W 165.78 FEET ALONG THE WEST LINE OF SAID SECTION 19; THENCE 421.20 FEET ALONG A 1535.89 FOOT RADIUS CURVE TO THE RIGHT, CHORD BEARING N75°48'09"E 419.88 FEET TO THE POINT OF BEGINNING; THENCE N83°47'49"E 14.00 FEET; THENCE 353.92 FEET ALONG A 2071.57 FOOT RADIUS CURVE TO THE RIGHT, CHORD BEARING S88°41'39"E 353.49 FEET; THENCE S83°47'59"E 686.21 FEET; THENCE S04°03'56"E 95.69 FEET TO THE NORTH 1/4 CORNER OF SAID SECTION 30; THENCE CONTINUING S04°03'56"E 969.72 FEET ALONG THE NORTH-SOUTH 1/4 LINE OF SAID SECTION 30; THENCE S86°19'40"W 1470.41 FEET; THENCE N03°14'44"W 683.33 FEET ALONG THE WEST LINE OF SAID SECTION 30; THENCE N86°22'06"E 424.02 FEET; THENCE N04°33'33"W 530.15 FEET TO THE POINT OF BEGINNING, CONTAINING 34.17 ACRES OF LAND, MORE OR LESS, AND SUBJECT EASEMENTS AND RESTRICTIONS OF RECORD, IF ANY.

# CONDITIONAL REZONING AGREEMENT City of South Lyon and Elevate Land Holdings LLC – Westlake (Vacant Parcel ID 21-30-126-003)

THIS CONDITIONAL REZONING AGREEMENT (the "Agreement") is made this ___ day of _____, 2024, by and between the CITY OF SOUTH LYON, a Michigan municipal corporation, with its offices located at 335 S. Warren Street, South Lyon, MI 48178 ("City") and Elevate Land Holdings, LLC- Westlake, a Delaware Limited Liability Company, whose address is 128 Center Street, Northville, MI, 48167 ("Owner").

## RECITALS

POINT OF BEGINNING; THENCE N83°47'49"E 14.00 FEET; THENCE 353.92 FEET ALONG A 2071.57 FOOT RADIUS CURVE TO THE RIGHT, CHORD BEARING S88°41'39"E 353.49 FEET; THENCE S83°47'59"E 686.21 FEET; THENCE S04°03'56"E 95.69 FEET TO THE NORTH 1/4 CORNER OF SAID SECTION 30; THENCE CONTINUING S04°03'56"E 969.72 FEET ALONG THE NORTH-SOUTH 1/4 LINE OF SAID SECTION 30; THENCE S86°19'40"W 1470.41 FEET; THENCE N03°14'44"W 683.33 FEET ALONG THE WEST LINE OF SAID SECTION 30; THENCE N86°22'06"E 424.02 FEET; THENCE N04°33'33"W 530.15 FEET TO THE POINT OF BEGINNING, CONTAINING 34.17 ACRES OF LAND, MORE OR LESS, AND SUBJECT EASEMENTS AND RESTRICTIONS OF RECORD, IF ANY.

WHEREAS, the Property is currently zoned I-1 (Light Industrial) and Owner desires to develop a mixed-use development to include a Multiple Family Residential and a Single Family Residential development project on a portion of the Property which is not permitted under the Property's I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district, and to develop a portion of the Property in conformance with the Property's existing I-1 Light Industrial zoning classification;

WHEREAS, pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, 2008 PA 110, MCL 125.3405 et seq., as amended, certain conditions voluntarily offered by the Owner of the Property, may become a condition of rezoning the Property;

WHEREAS, on or about June 6, 2023, a representative of the Owner, submitted a Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan, voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone the Property to PUD (Planned Unit Development);

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission held a public hearing, after publication of notice thereof, on the Owner's Conditional Rezoning Application to rezone the Property to PUD (Planned Unit Development);

WHEREAS, on September 28, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended approval of the Owner's request for conditional rezoning the Property to PUD (Planned Unit Development) based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by Owner in writing;

<a id="p13"></a>
*[Page 13 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=13)* (read by OCR; check the original)

WHEREAS, after receiving a favorable recommendation from the Planning Commission, but before the recommendation was presented to City Council, the Owner, City Planner, City Attorney and City Manager reevaluated the Owner's request to conditionally rezone to PUD (Planned Unit Development) and agreed that the conditional rezoning should have been to RM-3 (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 (Single Family Residential) for a portion of the Property.
WHEREAS, on or about November 9, 2023, a representative of the Owner submitted a revised Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone a portion of the Property from I-1 District (Light Industrial) to RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), and with conditions as set forth in this Agreement.
WHEREAS, on November 16, 2023, the City Planning Commission held a public hearing, after publication of notice thereof, on the Owner's Updated Conditional Rezoning Application to rezone a portion of the Property from I-1 District (Light Industrial) to RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential);
WHEREAS, on November 16, 2023, the City Planning Commission recommended approval of the Owner's request for conditional rezoning to rezone a portion of the Property from I-1 District (Light Industrial) to RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential) based upon the conditions voluntarily offered by Owner in writing which are set forth in this Agreement and the attached Exhibit A;
WHEREAS, on or about January 30, 2024, a representative of the Owner submitted a third revised Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone a portion of the Property from I-1

<a id="p14"></a>

District (Light Industrial) to RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), and with conditions as set forth in this Agreement.

WHEREAS, on or about April 30, 2024, a representative of the Owner submitted a fourth revised Conditional Rezoning Application for the Property along with a preliminary site plan, a written supplement, preliminary architectural drawings, and a preliminary landscape plan (attached as Exhibit A, collectively the "Updated Application Documents" and Exhibit B, the "Preliminary Site Plan") voluntarily offering, in writing, certain conditions to rezone a portion of the Property from I-1 District (Light Industrial) to RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), and with conditions as set forth in this Agreement.

WHEREAS, on May 13, 2024, the City Council considered the Updated Application Documents and Preliminary Site Plan, offered comment and approved the first reading the ordinance to amend the official zoning map of the City of South Lyon by conditionally rezoning portions of the Property from I-1 District (Light Industrial) to RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), subject to certain conditions;

WHEREAS, in response to comments received by City Council at the May 13, 2024 City Council Meeting, a representative of Owner submitted a revised Preliminary Site Plan, attached as Exhibit C (the "Revised Preliminary Site Plan"). The Revised Preliminary Site Plan reflects a shift in the pedestrian pathway throughout the development requiring de minimis changes to the legal description of the rezoned property (RM-3 District rezoning increased from 24.40 acres to 24.46 acres (+0.06 acre);

WHEREAS, on ___ 2024, the City Council approved the request for Conditional Rezoning and adopted Ordinance No. ___ based upon the conditions voluntarily

<a id="p16"></a>
*[Page 16 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=16)* (read by OCR; check the original)

offered by the Owner in writing which are set forth in this Agreement and the attached Exhibit A; and

NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the foregoing and the mutual promises hereinafter set forth, the City and Owner agree as follows:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Conditional Rezoning}} $. Pursuant to Section 405 of the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act, MCL 125.3405, Owner agrees to limit the development and use of the Property as set forth in the rezoning conditions below as a condition of rezoning. Upon execution of this Agreement by the parties, the City shall effectuate the conditional rezoning of the Residential Property from I-1 District (Limited Industrial) to the RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential) and R-3 District (Single Family Residential), as legally described herein, on the City's official Zoning Map. This Agreement is valid and entered into on a voluntary basis and represents a permissible exercise of authority by the City pursuant to MCL 125.3405.

2.  $ \underline{\text{Rezoning Conditions}} $. Owner agrees to develop and use the Property in accordance with the following conditions which have been offered voluntarily in writing by the Owner as conditions to the rezoning:

a. Development and use of the Property shall be for a phased development of Multiple Family Residential and Single Family Residential on the Residential Property, and a phased development of Industrial buildings on the remaining portion of the Property zoned I-1 District (Light Industrial) in substantial conformance with the revised preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit C, and in accordance with the Time Limits described in Paragraph 6, below, consisting of:

(1) 24.46\pm\frac{}{}-\ a c r e s\ of\ M u l t i p l e\ F a m i l y\ R e s i d e n t i a l\ P r o p e r t y,1.63\pm\frac{}{}-\ a c r e s\ of\ S i g l e\ F a m i l y\ R e s i d e n t i a l\ P r o p e r t y\ a n d\ 8.08\pm\frac{}{}-\ a c r e s\ of\ L i g h t\ I n d u s t r i a l\ P r o p e r t y;

(2) 252 Multiple Family 1-, 2-, and 3-bedroom residential units on the Property rezoned RM-3 District (Multiple Family Residential);

(3) 3 single family residential detached dwelling units on the Property rezoned R-3 District (Single Family Residential); and

(4) Approximately 64,000 square feet of industrial building space on the I-1 District (Light Industrial)

<a id="p17"></a>
*[Page 17 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=17)* (read by OCR; check the original)

(collectively referred to as the "Project"). The Project will be developed in the phases as depicted on the revised preliminary site plan attached as Exhibit C and as follows: Phase 1 of the Industrial development, shall include one 19,840 +/- sq. ft. industrial building; Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 4-7, 84 units total; Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 8-12, 84 units total, and the clubhouse with ancillary amenities; Phase 3 of the Multiple Family Residential shall include Buildings 13-18, 84 units total. The future development of the remaining I-1 (Light Industrial) zoned property and the R-3 (Single Family Residential) zoned property will be developed in accordance with the underlying zoning district.

b. Development of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development to occur concurrently.

c. Constructed as part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential development, the paving of 1,088 lineal feet of Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southerly end of the Property.

d. At the time of Dixboro Road paving, the pedestrian pathway ultimately connecting to Huron Valley Trail and to downtown South Lyon, will be installed from the southerly end of the Property and extending into the project to the extent possible within the limitations of the development of the future phases.

e. As part of Phase 1 of the Industrial development and Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential Development, a payment of $36,200 to the City of South Lyon will be made for the future construction of the remaining 724 lineal feet from the southerly Property line and Huron Valley Trail.

f. As a part of Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development, the pedestrian pathway will be completed in the manner as conceptually depicted in the attached Exhibit C, connecting the portion of the pedestrian pathway completed in Phase 1 and also connecting to the existing sidewalk that leads to downtown South Lyon.

g. Provided that all necessary permits are secured by the applicable state, county, city or other governmental agency, the existing Lake Street driveway approach serving Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) will be removed and relocated during Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential development in order to improve public and traffic safety. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

h. Owner shall secure and record all necessary shared access, use, utility, facilities, parking and other easements between the Project and adjacent development Westlake Place Apartments (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005) as required by and in a form approved by the City.

i. Minimum floor area reduction to allow for 1-bedroom units to be a minimum of 600 sq. ft., 2-bedroom units to be a minimum of 768 sq. ft. and 3-bedroom units to be

<a id="p18"></a>
*[Page 18 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=18)* (read by OCR; check the original)

a minimum of 936 sq. ft. to meet market demand for 1 - 3-bedroom units and to allow preservation of nine (9) acres of wetlands and 50% of the existing woodlands.

j. 568 parking spaces (which includes parking spaces within the existing Westlake Place Apartments, (Parcel ID 21-30-126-005), which will be available for use within the Project) provided for use by the Multiple Family Residential Property as recommended in the traffic study conducted by Fleis and Vandenbrink, which reduction will reduce impervious surfaces, increase available open space and area for community garden, recreation courts and a dog park.

k. 215 parking spaces provided for the industrial use to be located on the portion of the Property within the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning district. 67 parking spaces shall be provided for the Phase 1 Industrial development and the additional parking spaces are proposed because tenants and/or users have not been identified or secured for the Industrial use. Once a site plan is submitted for the specific configuration of the remaining Light Industrial district, the City shall determine the required number of parking spaces required or shall cause the Owner to conduct a parking and/or traffic study to establish a basis for the City to consider the appropriate number of parking spaces required. If an amendment to the final approved site plan is required, it shall be considered a minor revision.

For the efficient use of the Property, considering the Project meets several master plan goals, the buffering requirement of the City's Zoning Ordinance between industrial and residential uses must be waived and, once tenants are secured and a site plan submitted, the City shall determine the appropriate buffer requirement considering the proposed industrial use.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Use of the Property}} $. Development and use of the Property shall be in substantial conformance with the approved preliminary site plan, attached as Exhibit C, and shall be subject to and in conformance with the approval of the final site plan for the Property by the City Planning Commission. The parties agree that minor revisions to the approved final site plan which, in the sole discretion of the City Manager, do not significantly modify the proposed development or use of the Property or the site plan, may be considered and approved by the City administratively without the necessity of review and approval by the Planning Commission and/or without amendment of this Agreement and exhibits hereto.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Laws}} $. Except as modified herein, development and use of the Property shall comply with all other applicable state, county and local requirements for land

<a id="p19"></a>
*[Page 19 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=19)* (read by OCR; check the original)

development and use, ordinances, codes, standards, regulations, requirements, and engineering design standards and requirements.

5.  $ \underline{\text{Compliance with Conditions}} $. Once commenced and established, the development and use of the Property must remain in compliance with the conditions of the conditional rezoning Final Site Plan and as provided in this Agreement. Any failure to comply with a condition contained herein shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance and shall be punishable as provided for therein. Additionally, any such violation shall be deemed a nuisance per se and subject to abatement as provided for by law.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Time Limits}} $. The City and Owner agree that the following approvals and activities are conditions to this Agreement and if not completed within the following time limits, the Residential Property may revert back to the I-1 (Light Industrial) zoning classification:

a. Final site plan approval within twelve (12) months from the date of this Agreement;

b. A building permit for the Phase 1 Multiple Family Residential and the Phase 1 Industrial development thereof, if applicable, is obtained within one (1) year from the date of final site plan approval;

c. A building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 1 of the Multiple Family Residential.

d. A building permit for Phase 3 of the Multiple-Family Residential is obtained within twenty-four (24) months of the issuance of the first building permit for Phase 2 of the Multiple Family Residential.

e. A final certificate of occupancy for the entire Multiple Family Residential, or Phase 2, or Phase 3 thereof if applicable, is obtained within thirty (30) months from the date the building permit was issued for said Phase.

7.  $ \underline{\text{Extension of Time Limits}} $. The City, in the City's exclusive discretion, may agree to extend the time limits in Paragraph 6 upon receipt of a written request from the Owner but the City shall not be required to grant such request for extension. Any extension granted shall be in writing signed by both parties.

<a id="p20"></a>
*[Page 20 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=20)* (read by OCR; check the original)

8.  $ \underline{\text{City Right to Rezone}} $. In the event that the time limit conditions in Paragraph 6 are not met, nor extended upon mutual agreement of the parties pursuant to Paragraph 7; nothing in this Agreement shall be deemed to prohibit the City from rezoning all or any portion of the Property which is subject to this Agreement to another zoning district. Any rezoning shall be conducted in compliance with the City's Zoning Ordinance and the Michigan Zoning Enabling Act.

9. Owner Acknowledgments. Owner acknowledges that it voluntarily offered the conditions contained in this Agreement, including all Exhibits, in connection with the request for conditional rezoning of the Property and that such conditions were offered in writing. Owner acknowledges that upon the approval of the requested conditional rezoning and this Agreement becoming effective, the development and use of the Property shall conform thereafter with the terms and conditions of the conditional rezoning, this Agreement, and all the requirements regulating development and use within the zoning district as modified by the voluntarily offered conditions and with this Agreement and that any failure to fully comply with such conditions and/or this Agreement shall constitute a violation of the City of South Lyon Zoning Ordinance, and shall be punishable accordingly. Owner acknowledges and agrees that the terms and conditions of this Agreement are necessary and roughly proportional to the burden imposed by the conditional rezoning of the Property, and are therefore necessary to ensure that public services and facilities will be capable of accommodating the development and use and the increased service of facility loads caused by the development and use, to protect the natural environment and conserve natural resources, to ensure compatibility with adjacent uses of land, and to promote the use of the Property in a socially and economically feasible manner, and to achieve other legitimate objectives authorized by law. Owner agrees that the terms and conditions herein do not constitute a taking of property for any purpose or a violation of any Constitutional

<a id="p21"></a>
*[Page 21 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=21)* (read by OCR; check the original)

right, and Owner agrees to be bound by each and every provision and conditions of this Agreement. Owner acknowledges and agrees that if the conditional rezoning becomes void for any reason, then no further development shall take place and no permits shall be issued unless and until a new zoning district classification for the Property has been established.

10.  $ \underline{\text{Entire Agreement}} $. This Agreement and Exhibits, together with any agreements referenced herein, constitutes the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject of this Agreement.

11.  $ \underline{\text{Binding Effect, Running with the Land}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on and

inure to the benefit of the parties and their respective successors, heirs, assigns and transferees,

and shall run with the land.

12.  $ \underline{\text{Amendments}} $. The terms of this Agreement may be amended, changed, or modified, but only by written agreement executed by the required parties. Any amendment of this Agreement shall be recorded in the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

13.  $ \underline{\text{Governing Law}} $. This Agreement shall be governed by, construed, and enforced in accordance with Michigan law.

14.  $ \underline{\text{Authority to Execute}} $. The parties each represent and state that the individuals

signing this Agreement are fully authorized to execute this document and bind their respective

parties to the terms and conditions contained herein.

15.  $ \underline{\text{Enforcement}} $. In the event of a proceeding to enforce this Agreement, a prevailing party may seek to recover its costs and attorney fees in addition to any other applicable and available relief.

16.  $ \underline{\text{Joint Drafting}} $. No provision of this Agreement shall be construed against or interpreted to the disadvantage of one party against any other party by any court or other

<a id="p22"></a>
*[Page 22 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=22)* (read by OCR; check the original)

governmental authority by reason of any determination or assertion that one party was chiefly or primarily responsible for having drafted this Agreement.

17.  $ \underline{\text{Conflicts}} $. In the event of conflict between the provisions of this Agreement and the provisions of another applicable ordinance, code, regulations, requirement, standard, or policy, the provisions of this Agreement shall prevail.

18.  $ \underline{\text{Severability}} $. This invalidity of any provision of this Agreement shall not affect the validity of the remaining provisions, which shall remain valid and enforceable to the fullest extent permitted by law.

19.  $ \underline{\text{Recording}} $. This Agreement shall be binding on the parties and the Property and shall be recorded with the Oakland County Register of Deeds.

 $ \underline{\text{Counterparts}} $. This Agreement and any amendments to it may be executed in multiple counterparts, each of which shall be deemed an original and all of which shall constitute one Agreement. The signature of any party to any counterpart shall be deemed to be a signature to, and may be appended to, any other counterpart.

IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have executed this Agreement on the date first set forth above.

City of South Lyon

Planning Commission and Zoning Board of Appeals

335 S. Warren Street

South Lyon, MI 48178

RE: Westlake

To Whom It May Concern,

We are very excited to submit the attached applications and plans for the project we are calling Westlake. Please accept this supplement to our Site Plan Review and Rezoning Application.

This application includes the current project known as Westlake Place Apartments, and the primary addition of the Industrially zoned property to the South and East, commonly known as the 'Chuck Smith' property or 'West End Industrial'. The existing Industrial property is vacant and has been vacant for quite some time. We believe this vacancy is due to several reasons, which we are attempting to address and resolve in the present application. At the same time, we are proposing an important addition to the housing supply within the City, in a market segment that is substantially underserved. Our application proposes a combination of uses that we feel balances these needs, provides for a healthy transition between them, and provides the City with important public amenities that benefit the entire community.

Below are supplemental comments to the site plan review and rezoning applications for your reference and in an effort explain our application more completely.

Why, in our opinion, the requested rezoning is necessary for the presentation and enjoyment of substantial property rights, and why such change will not be detrimental to the public welfare, nor the property of other persons located in the vicinity:

We are proposing to contribute to the housing needs within the City and satisfy the industrial needs within the City with the addition of 3 single family residence parcels, 255 multifamily units and the retention of an appropriate level of industrially zoned land. We believe that this proposal reflects a balance of industrial and multi-family residential that prudently allocates each according to the outstanding demand for both. The proposed industrial provides a perfect transition to the adjacent, existing heavy industrial (Michigan Seamless Tube). The multi-family component borders the industrial, with an enhanced transition including garages / storage areas and a substantially landscaped perimeter further separating the residential units from the industrial property.

To the north, the property is bordered by residential areas, predominantly the Charleston Park subdivision. Our proposed main entrance from West Lake Street lines up directly across from the entrance to Charleston Park. The entrance location is ideal from a public safety, traffic management, aesthetic, and natural resource conversation perspective. The site is designed to minimize impact to residents of Charleston Park. Our proposed entrance opens to a captivating view of the club house, landscaping, and amenities. Units within the site are set back substantially from West Lake Street, across a wetland and a wooded buffer.

<a id="p29"></a>
*[Page 29 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=29)* (read by OCR; check the original)

To the west is Green Oak Township. The Dixboro Road frontage is largely existing developed (currently Westlake Place Apartments) and our proposal enhances the existing entrance with a boulevard entrance to the multi-family phase, and enhanced retention pond with the intention of maintaining surface water. The proposed industrial building is perpendicular to Dixboro Road, and extra attention is given to the road design and curb appeal.

Explain how the requested rezoning is consistent with the goals, policies, and Future Land Use Map of the City of South Lyon Master Plan, including any sub-area or corridor studies. If conditions have changed since the Plan was adopted, the consistency with recent development trends in the area:

The proposed rezoning fills a long-standing desire for this Property to be industrially developed. This property has been zoned, partially developed, and vacant for almost 25 years. Our proposal advances that intention in part, and importantly, to the degree of absorption that is appropriate for the City of South Lyon.

Equally important is the City of South Lyon's need for affordable, non-owner occupied, Missing Middle housing for professionals and upwardly mobile singles. This demand is partially met in the recently developed existing Westlake Place Apartments, which are fully absorbed and stabilized. Our expansion comes closer to filling that need and providing the citizens of South Lyon with more upscale affordable housing options with a rich amenity mix tailored to modern lifestyle desires.

The uses must have a beneficial effect, in terms of public health, safety, welfare, or convenience, on present and future potential surrounding land uses. The uses proposed must not adversely affect the public utility and circulation system, surrounding properties, or the environment. The public benefit shall be one which could not be achieved under the regulations of the underlying district alone or that of any other zoning district.

Beneficial effects to the public and to surrounding land uses of our proposal include:

Paving Dixboro Road from Lake Street to the southern property line, approximately 1088' from the corner has a clear and obvious effect on public health, safety welfare and convenience to our residents and to the Industrial users and their customers.

Paving a connection to the existing sidewalk along Lake Street, dramatically improving non-motorized accessibility to the City downtown for our residents and neighborhoods.

Paving a connection between development and the Huron Valley Rail Trail (entire length of Dixboro Road from West Lake Street to trail head), dramatically improving accessibility to residents and neighborhoods. (This is already a requirement under the current Westlake Place development)

Public safety is enhanced by increasing / creating non-motorized circulation without forcing road crossings.

Removal of existing West Lake Street entrance to Westlake Place Apartments and creating new entrance creates a much safer traffic flow environment and is aesthetically superior for a host of reasons.

Placement of 'Welcome to South Lyon' sign at the corner of West Lake Street and Dixboro Road will enhance 10 Mile corridor from the west.

We hope you share our view that this application a fantastic opportunity to put a long-dormant property to use, provide the city with a healthy balance of industrial/business flex space and multi-family residents and enhance the Lake Street / Dixboro entrance to the City.

Very Truly Yours,

*[Page 34 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=34)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Executive Summary

Introduction – This interim and draft Executive Summary supports a Residential Target Market Analysis prepared for Elevate Land Holdings, LLC (hereinafter, "Elevate") in the Summer of 2023. The deliverables have included four documents as follows: 1) Residential Target Market Analysis; 2) Real Estate Analysis; 3) Demographic Analysis; and 4) Economic Analysis.

This narrative focuses primarily on the Residential Target Market Analysis, hereinafter the "housing study". Excerpts from the complete study have been assembled into a relatively short Handout for distribution to the City of South Lyon's Planning Commission. One of the exhibits (a scatter plot) in the Handout also have been excerpted from the Real Estate Analysis. Electronic copies of all four documents can be made available to the City upon request, recognizing that additional narrative reports are still underway.

Project Description – The Westlake PUD site includes roughly 40 acres located at the southeast quadrant of 10 Mile Road (W Lake Street) and Dixboro Road in the City of South Lyon, Michigan. Nearby uses include an industrial mill to the southeast and detached houses to the north and west (generally on cul-de-sacs). The northern entrance into the project is ideally located just 0.7 miles west of South Lyon's downtown.

If approved as envisioned, the total Westlake PUD project eventually would include three phases of apartment buildings, with Phase One already completed (72 units), and Phase Two (144 units) and Phase Three (180 units) proposed. The latter two phases would collectively add about 324 new apartment units, ideally over the course of three years. A clubhouse, pool, and spacious dog park also are proposed as amenities to help intercept migrating households. Detached garages might be available for some of the units, and extra storage space also is being explored.

LandUseUSA toured the South Lyon market, visited the downtown, and conducted a site visit in mid-2023. Based on the results of the market analysis (with additional details on the following pages) and the site assessment, we are in support of Elevate's Westlake PUD residential and industrial mixed-use project and recommend that the developer proceed with confidence that the rents can meet and perhaps even exceed expectations. Rent will easily be 10% higher than Phase One, and up to 20% higher than city-wide averages.

We also recommend that Elevate target single professionals of all ages, with incomes that can tolerate minimum rents of $1,350 per month for studio apartments up to $2,400 per month for corner units with two bedrooms. Finally, we recommend that the industrial component of the project be no larger than 65,000 square feet and no smaller than 65,000 square feet. In other words, the size of that proposed industrial space is just right.

<a id="p35"></a>
*[Page 35 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=35)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Market Potential

City-Wide Market Potential – Based on the results of the housing study, about 285 new renter households are migrating into the City of South Lyon each year, unadjusted for out-migration. If new for-lease units can be built in the city, then it will not only intercept these new renters, but also help intercept out-migration, resulting in net population growth.

In addition to in-migration, about 110 existing renter households are migrating within South Lyon each year by moving from one address to another within the same city. Lastly, about 55 households are bypassing the city each year but could potentially be intercepted if new for-lease choices become available. Collectively, these three components represent a maximum, not-to-exceed market potential for 450 new for-lease units throughout the city each year. See below for a table summary.

<div>Summary of the Annual Market Potential For-Lease New-Build Units The City of South Lyon, Michigan</div>

<table><tr><td>Summary</td><td>Type of</td><td>Total</td><td>Apt</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Potential</td><td>Movership</td><td>Units</td><td>Units</td><td>Strategy</td></tr><tr><td>Capture</td><td>In-Migration</td><td>285</td><td>100</td><td>Cautious</td></tr><tr><td>Retain</td><td>Internal Migration</td><td>110</td><td>35</td><td>Bold</td></tr><tr><td>Intercept</td><td>Bypassing Migration</td><td>55</td><td>20</td><td>Aggressive</td></tr><tr><td>Maximum</td><td>Aggregate Migration</td><td>450</td><td>155</td><td></td></tr></table>

Of the 450 renter households migrating into, within, and bypassing the city each year, only 155 of them will be inclined to lease an apartment or urban loft. The balance will either seek townhouses with private entrances and small porches (45 households); and detached units like subdivided houses, cottages, and/or accessory dwellings (250 households).

Similarly, of the 450 renter households migrating into, within, and bypassing the city each year, only one-third will tolerate rents that are in the same threshold as Westlake PUD's current units (about $1,300 and above). This generally aligns with the assessment of formats similarly points to a limit of about 155 units annually in Phase Two and Phase Three of the Westlake PUD.

<a id="p36"></a>
*[Page 36 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=36)* (read by OCR; check the original)

It also is important to note that if new competing apartment complexes are developed at the same time as the Westlake PUD, then they would need to share the maximum annual market potential for 155 units. To stay on the safe side, Westlake PUD probably should plan on building no more than 100 units in any given year, over the course of three years until build-out is achieved.

In terms of price, only one-third of the migrating renter households will tolerate rents of $1,300 per month or more. There also is a segment of the market that would tolerate higher rents up to $1,700 per month, and a few households will even tolerate prices up to $2,300. Households that can tolerate rents above $2,300 will either look for houses (or luxury townhouses) to lease; or they could be converted into home buyers.

## Real Estate Analysis

This housing study includes a detailed Real Estate Analysis that compares the asking rents, square feet, and rent per square foot among existing housing choices throughout the City of South Lyon, the four surrounding townships (Green Oak, Lyon, Salem, and Northfield townships, in clockwise order), and some nearby places like Brighton and Wixom. Based on the results, there is an upside potential for the Westlake PUD to increase monthly contract rents on new-build units in Phases Two and Three (compared to existing units in Phase One) by about +$125 per square foot for the smallest units, and +$150 per square foot for the largest units.

The smallest one-bedroom apartments with 600 square feet could have rents of about $1,500 per month; one-bedroom units with 700 square feet could have rents of $1,575 per month; small two-bedroom units with 800 square feet could have rents of $1,680 per month; and larger two-bedroom units with 1,000 square feet could have rents approaching $2,000 per month.

Although smaller units tend to have lower monthly rents, they have higher rents per square foot. In other words, smaller units are more efficient, whereas the rents per square foot progressively erode for larger units. Smaller units also are more likely to appeal to singles with one wage-earner, and especially the “Bohemian Groove”, “Full Steam Ahead”, “Striving Single” target markets. Units with lower rents also tend to have the highest absorption rates.

However, we also recognize that the City of South Lyon is high-quality market and a destination for families, such as the "Family Troopers". Therefore, we have recommended that Elevate strive for a good balance between one-bedroom, two-bedroom, and three-bedroom units. We also have recommended that the new units be provided with larger balconies than Phase One.

<a id="p37"></a>
*[Page 37 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=37)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Industrial Component

Industrial Component – The proposed Westlake PUD project also includes four light industrial buildings with a collective 63,000 to 65,000 square feet located along the southern edge of the site. Based on the Elevate's preliminary concepts, potential light industrial tenants include research & development, shipping & receiving, machine shops, professional contractors, and other small businesses that are more inclined to lease than own space. Note: The Beck Business Center (located at the northwest quadrant of I-96 and Beck Road in nearby Wixom) has been discussed as possible analog.

Elevate has asked LandUseUSA to comment on the optimal size and mix of uses in the industrial component of the Westlake PUD. Based on our market assessment and field observations, we believe that the project could attract some small level of interest from non-industrial small businesses like a microbrewery, fitness center, health care practitioners, veterinarian, marketing agencies, insurance agencies, and attorney offices. However, many of these uses might also be interested in locating at the fringes of Downtown South Lyon or along the Pontiac Trail corridor.

Based on our assessment of the site's locational attributes, and especially visibility to vehicular traffic, we are concerned that a smaller project would struggle to survive without the necessary critical mass. On the flip side, a larger project could run the risk of siphoning small businesses away from the downtown.

Lastly, a larger project might struggle to attract enough quality tenants to fill the space, resulting in high vacancy, which in turn could undermines rents and profitability. Note: Vacancy rates will be less of a concern for other projects that are located Pontiac Trail and that have better exposure to vehicular traffic.

For the reasons described above, LandUseUSA recommends that Elevate proceed with the Industrial component of the project, but with caution. Good occupancy rates and rents can be optimized with a flexible design that appeals to a variety of businesses and that also is adaptable over time and to each tenant's unique needs. In short, 65,000 square feet of space feels about right – anything less would be too small, and anything more would be too large.

Prepared August 6, 2023

Sharon Woods, President

Sharon M. Woods

LandUseUSA | Urban Strategies

# Half of all Lifestyle Clusters | Nationwide A01 – J36 | Better-to-Upper Incomes

Ao1 | American Royalty - Wealthy influential couples and families in prestigious communities - Suburbs.

Ao2 | Platinum Prosperity - Wealthy and established empty-nesting couples - Suburbs.

A03 | Children & Cabernet - Prosperous, middle-aged married couples focused on their children's lives - Suburbs.

Ao4 | Picture Perfect Families - Established families of child-raising households in wealthy communities - Suburbs.

A05 | Couples with Clout - Middle-aged childless couples living in affluent areas - Metros.

Ao6 | Jet Set Urbanites - Mix of affluent singles and couples enjoying diverse neighborhoods - Urban.

Bo7 | Generational Soup - Affluent couples and multi-generational families, wide range of lifestyles - Suburbs.

Bo8 | Babies & Bliss - Middle-aged couples with large families and active lives - Suburbs.

Bog | Family Funtastic - Upscale, middle-aged families with busy lives focused on older children - Satellite Cities.

B10 | Cosmopolitan Achievers - Affluent middle-aged, established couples & families, dynamic lifestyles - Metros.

C11 | Aging of Aquarius, Settled - Upscale boomer couples settled in detached houses - Cities, Nearby Suburbs.

C12 | Golf Carts & Gourmets - Upscale retirees & empty-nesters in comfortable golf communities - Urban Edges.

C13 | Silver Sophisticates - Mature, upscale couples & singles in larger detached houses - Suburbs.

C14 | Boomers & Boomerangs - Baby boomer adults with young adult children sharing their house - Suburbs.

D15 | Sports Utility Families - Upscale, multi-generational, middle-aged families, active lifestyles - Outer Suburbs.

D16 | Settled in Suburbia - Upper-middle-income diverse families & empty nesters - Established Suburbs.

D17 | Cul de Sac Diversity - Culturally diverse, middle-aged families settling into emerging communities - Suburbs.

D18 | Suburban Attainment - Upper middle-class couples and families moving to newer communities - Suburbs.

E19 | Full Pockets & Empty Nests - Empty-nesters, discretionary income and sophisticated lifestyles - Most Cities.

E2o | No Place Like Home - Middle-to-upper income, multi-generational households, detached houses - Urban Edges.

E21 | Unspoiled Splendor - Comfortably established baby boomer couples, detached houses - Small Cities, Rural.

F22 | Fast Track Couples - Young, upwardly-mobile couples with active lifestyles - Inner Suburbs.

F23 | Families Matter Most - Young, middle-to-upper income families with active, family-focused lives - Suburbs.

G24 | Status Seeking Singles - Young, upwardly-mobile singles balancing work and leisure - Metros, Urban.

G25 | Urban Edge - Younger, up-and-coming singles living big-city lifestyles - Largest Metros.

H26 | Progressive Potpourri - Mature couples with comfortable and active lives - Suburbs.

H27 | Birkenstocks & Beemers - Middle-to-upper income couples living leisurely lifestyles - Small Cities.

H28 | Everyday Moderates - Multi-cultural couples & families choosing modest lifestyles - Suburbs to Mid-sized Cities.

H29 | Destination Recreation - Middle-aged couples working hard to support active lifestyles - Small Cities, Suburbs.

130 | Stockcars & State Parks - Middle-income couples & families seeking affordable entertainment - Small Cities.

131 | Blue Collar Comfort - Middle-income families working solid, blue-collar jobs - Small Cities.

## 71 Lifestyle Clusters - General Approach Experian Decision Analytics; 2020 - 2023

More than 300 data points have been used to build Mosaic USA. These have been selected as inputs to the classification on the basis of their coverage, quality, consistency and sustainability.

The data variables enable accurate identification and differentiation between a wide range of consumer characteristics and attributes. (See the list below.)

The data variables are updated quarterly to ensure continued accuracy in assignments of the Mosaic codes.

In general, they meet the following criteria:

In general, they meet the following criteria:

- Allow the identification and description of consumer segments that are not necessarily distinguished solely by the use of census data.

Ensure accuracy of Mosaic code by either household or neighborhood.

• Are updated regularly to ensure that changes are monitored.

- Improve differentiation and allow for the identification of a wide range of consumer behaviors.

## 1 INTRODUCTION

This memorandum presents the results of the Traffic Impact Study (TIS) for the proposed West Lake Planned Unit Development (PUD) in the City of South Lyon, Michigan. The project site is generally located in the southeast quadrant of the 10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road intersection, as shown on the attached Figure 1.

The purpose of this study is to provide a TIS and Parking Study for the West Lake PUD, as currently proposed. The existing West Lake PUD Phase 1 has been completed and Phases 2, 3, and 4 are currently proposed. Site access for Phase 1 was previously constructed and provided via one (1) site driveway on Dixboro Road and one (1) site driveway on 10-Mile Road. As part of this project, the existing site access on 10-Mile Road will be closed and a new shared driveway will be constructed on 10-Mile Road, aligned directly opposite of the existing Singh Boulevard. Additional site access is proposed via two (2) new driveways on Dixboro Road. The study roadways are under the jurisdiction of the Road Commission for Oakland County (RCOC). The City of South Lyon has required a TIS for this project as part of the PUD approval process.

The scope of the study was developed based on Fleis & VandenBrink's (F&V) understanding of the development program, accepted traffic engineering practice, the requirements of the City of South Lyon and RCOC, and professional experience. The study intersection analyses were completed using Synchro/SimTraffic (Version 11) traffic analysis software. Sources of data for this study include F&V subconsultant Quality Counts (QC), information published by the Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE), the City of South Lyon, the Southeast Michigan Council of Governments (SEMCOG), and RCOC.

## 2 BACKGROUND

### 2.1 EXISTING ROAD NETWORK

The lane uses and traffic control at the study intersections are shown on the attached Figure 2 and the study roadways are further described below. For the purposes of this study, site driveways and residential streets were assumed to have an operating speed of 25 miles per hour (mph) unless otherwise noted.

10-Mile Road (W. Lake Street) generally runs in the east and west directions, adjacent to the north side of the project site. The study section of 10-Mile Road is under the jurisdiction of RCOC, is classified as a Minor Arterial, has an Average Annual Daily Traffic (AADT) volume of approximately 9,000 (SEMCOG 2018) vehicles per day (vpd), and provides a typical two-lane cross-section, with one (1) lane of travel in each direction. 10-Mile Road has posted speed limits of 35-mph and 45-mph, to the east and west of Dixboro Road, respectively.

27725 Stansbury Boulevard, Suite 195

P: 248.536.0080

F: 248.536.0079

<a id="p76"></a>
*[Page 76 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=76)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Dixboro Road runs in the north and south directions, adjacent to the west side of the project site. Dixboro Road is under the jurisdiction of RCOC, is classified as a Major Collector, has a prima facie speed limit of 55-mph, and has an AADT volume of approximately 500 vpd (SEMCOG 2018). The study section of Dixboro Road is unpaved and provides a typical two-lane cross section, with one (1) lane of travel in each direction.

Edenderry Drive generally runs in the east and west directions, aligned directly opposite the proposed industrial driveway. Edenderry Drive is classified as a Local Road, is under the jurisdiction of RCOC, and provides a typical two-lane cross-section, with one (1) lane of travel in each direction. The roadway does not provide a posted speed limit; therefore, a prima facie residential speed limit of 25-mph was assumed.

### 2.2 EXISTING TRAFFIC VOLUMES

F&V subconsultant QC collected existing Turning Movement Count (TMC) data on March 21st, 2023, during the AM (7:00 AM to 9:00 AM) and PM (4:00 PM to 6:00 PM) peak periods, at the following study intersections:

10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road

## Dixboro Road & Edenderry Road

During collection of the turning movement counts, Peak Hour Factors (PHFs) and commercial truck percentages were recorded and used in the traffic analysis. The peak hour traffic volumes for each intersection were utilized for this study; the volumes were balanced upward through the study network and balanced through the proposed site driveways. Therefore, the raw traffic volumes shown in the data collection may not match the traffic volumes used in the analysis and on the attached traffic volume figures.

The weekday AM and PM peak hours for the adjacent roadway network were observed to generally occur between 7:00 AM to 8:00 AM and 4:30 PM to 5:30 PM, respectively. F&V collected an inventory of existing lane use and traffic controls, as shown on the attached Figure 2.

## 3 EXISTING CONDITIONS (2023)

Existing peak hour vehicle delays and Levels of Service (LOS) were calculated at the study intersections using Synchro/SimTraffic (Version 11) traffic analysis software. The study analyses were based on the existing lane use and traffic control shown on the attached Figure 2, the existing peak hour traffic volumes shown on the attached Figure 3, and the methodologies presented in the Highway Capacity Manual.  $ 6^{th} $ Edition (HCM6).

Descriptions of LOS "A" through "F" as defined in the HCM6, are attached. Typically, LOS D is considered acceptable, with LOS A representing minimal delay, and LOS F indicating failing conditions. Additionally, SimTraffic network simulations were reviewed to evaluate network operations and vehicle queues. The existing conditions results are attached and summarized in Table 1.

<div>Table 1: Existing Intersection Operations</div>

<table><tr><td rowspan="2" colspan="2">Intersection</td><td rowspan="2">Control</td><td rowspan="2">Approach</td><td colspan="4">Existing Conditions</td></tr><tr><td>AM Peak Delay (s/veh)</td><td>PM Peak Delay (s/veh)</td><td>LOS</td><td>LOS</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="3">1</td><td rowspan="3">10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road</td><td rowspan="3">Stop (Minor)</td><td>EB</td><td colspan="4">Free</td></tr><tr><td>WBL</td><td>9.3</td><td>A</td><td>8.2</td><td>A</td></tr><tr><td>NB</td><td>17.2</td><td>C</td><td>15.2</td><td>C</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="4">2</td><td rowspan="4">Dixboro Road & Edenderry Drive / Industrial Site Drive</td><td rowspan="4">Stop (Minor)</td><td>EB</td><td>9.5</td><td>A</td><td>9.4</td><td>A</td></tr><tr><td>WB</td><td>$ 0.0^{{*}} $</td><td>$ A^{{*}} $</td><td>$ 0.0^{{*}} $</td><td>$ A^{{*}} $</td></tr><tr><td>NBL</td><td>7.5</td><td>A</td><td>7.3</td><td>A</td></tr><tr><td>SBL</td><td>0.0</td><td>A</td><td>0.0</td><td>A</td></tr></table>

 $ ^{*} $Indicates no vehicle volume present

The result of the existing conditions analysis indicates that all approaches and movements at the study intersections are currently operating acceptably, at LOS D or better, during both the AM and PM peak periods.

A review of SimTraffic network simulations also indicates acceptable operations throughout the study roadway network during both peak periods.

## 4 BACKGROUND (2027) CONDITIONS

Population and economic growth profile data for the City of South Lyon was obtained from the Southeast Michigan Council of Governments (SEMCOG) database to calculate a background growth rate to project the 2023 peak hour traffic volumes to the site buildout year of 2027. Population and employment projections from 2020 to 2050 were reviewed and showed an average annual growth of 0.32% and 0.45%, respectively. Therefore, a conservative annual background growth rate of 0.5% per year was applied to the existing peak hour traffic volumes to forecast the background 2027 peak hour traffic volumes, without the proposed development. Additionally, it is important to account for developments within the study network, which will be constructed prior to the site buildout year of 2027, the following development was identified within the study network:

## Charleston Park: 195 unit Single-Family Detached

The number of weekday peak hour (AM and PM) and daily vehicle trips that would be generated by the Charleston Park development were forecast based on data published by ITE in the Trip Generation Manual,  $ 11^{th} $ Edition. The background development trip generation projections are summarized in Table 2.

<div>Table 2: Charleston Park Trip Generation Summary</div>

<table><tr><td rowspan="2">Land Use</td><td rowspan="2">ITE Code</td><td rowspan="2">Amount</td><td rowspan="2">Units</td><td rowspan="2">Average Daily Traffic (vpd)</td><td colspan="3">AM Peak Hour (vph)</td><td colspan="3">PM Peak Hour (vph)</td></tr><tr><td>In</td><td>Out</td><td>Total</td><td>In</td><td>Out</td><td>Total</td></tr><tr><td>Single Family Detached</td><td>210</td><td>195</td><td>DU</td><td>1,865</td><td>34</td><td>103</td><td>137</td><td>117</td><td>69</td><td>186</td></tr></table>

The proposed Charleston Park neighborhood is partially occupied and is currently under construction for the remaining phases. In order to provide a conservative analysis of the study roadway network, the development was assumed to be unoccupied; the full trip generation for the neighborhood was then projected and distributed onto the study roadway network. Therefore, the existing peak hour traffic volumes were combined with the background development site-generated trips, after applying the calculated background growth rate of 0.5%, in order to forecast the background (2027) traffic volumes without the proposed development.

Future peak hour vehicle delays and LOS without the proposed development were calculated based on the existing lane use and traffic control shown on the attached Figure 2, the background traffic volumes shown on the attached Figure 4, and the methodologies presented in the HCM6. The results of the analysis of background conditions are attached and summarized in Table 3.

The results of the background conditions analysis indicate that all approaches and movements at the study intersection will continue to operate acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to existing conditions, with minor increases in delays. Additionally, review of SimTraffic microsimulations indicates acceptable operations during both peak periods, with minimal vehicle queueing observed throughout the study roadway network.

## 5 SITE TRIP GENERATION

The number of weekday peak hour (AM and PM) and daily vehicle trips that would be generated by the proposed West Lake PUD were forecast based on data published by ITE in the Trip Generation Manual, 11th Edition. Part of the proposed development has already been constructed and is currently occupied; however, in order to provide a conservative evaluation, the full buildout of the proposed PUD was projected. The trip generation projections are summarized in Table 4.

<div>Table 4: Trip Generation Summary (Proposed PUD)</div>

## 6 SITE TRIP DISTRIBUTION

The vehicular trips that would be generated by the proposed development were assigned to the study roadway network based on the proposed site access plan and driveway configurations, the existing peak hour traffic patterns in the adjacent roadway network, and the methodologies published by ITE. New trips were assumed to enter the network and access the development, then leave the development and return to their direction of origin. The site trip distributions utilized in this analysis are summarized in Table 5.

The site-generated trips shown on the attached Figure 5, were added to the background peak hour traffic volumes shown on the attached Figure 4, in order to calculate the future peak hour traffic volumes, as shown on the attached Figure 6.

## 7 FUTURE CONDITIONS

The future peak hour vehicle delays and LOS, with the addition of the proposed development, was based on the proposed lane use and traffic control shown on the attached Figure 2, the proposed site access plan, the future peak hour traffic volumes shown on the attached Figure 6, and the methodologies presented in the HCM6. The results of the future conditions analysis are attached and summarized in Table 6.

The results of the future conditions analysis indicate that all study intersection approaches and movements will continue to operate acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to the background conditions analysis, with the exception of the following:

## 10 -Mile Road & Dixboro Road

•  $ \underline{\text{During the AM peak hour}} $: The northbound approach is expected to operate at LOS E.

Although the intersection LOS analysis indicates poor operations for the northbound approach during the AM peak period, a review of SimTraffic microsimulations indicates acceptable operations. SimTraffic network

<a id="p79"></a>
*[Page 79 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=79)* (read by OCR; check the original)

simulations indicate that vehicles on the northbound approach were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing. The  $ 95^{th} $ percentile vehicle queue length reported for the northbound approach during the AM peak period was approximately 82-feet (3-4 vehicles), which is not significant.

## 10 -Mile Road & Site Drive #1 / Singh Boulevard

- During the PM peak hour: The northbound and southbound left-turn movements are expected to operate at LOS E.

Although the intersection LOS analysis indicates poor operations associated with the minor street left-turn movements during the PM peak period, a review of SimTraffic microsimulations indicates acceptable operations. SimTraffic network simulations indicate that vehicles on the northbound and southbound left-turn movements were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing. The 95th percentile vehicle queue length reported for the northbound and southbound left-turn movement was approximately 53-feet (2-3 vehicles) or less, which is not significant. Additionally, aligning the proposed Site Drive #1 directly opposite Singh Boulevard follows best access management practices.

## 8 PARKING ANALYSIS

The proposed development plan for the West Lake PUD site was evaluated to determine the recommended number of parking spaces for the residential uses of the site. The Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE) and the South Lyon parking ordinances were both used for this analysis.

The Institute of Transportation Engineers (ITE) Parking Generation, 5th Edition was used to determine the parking demand for this site. The ITE Parking Generation is an informational guide used by engineers and planners for the purposes of determining the parking demand associated with various land uses. The parking generation data included in Parking Generation are provided by various state and local government agencies.

<a id="p80"></a>
*[Page 80 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=80)* (read by OCR; check the original)

consulting firms, individual transportation professionals, universities, developers, associations, local sections, districts, and student chapters of ITE located throughout the U.S. The data is examined by ITE for validity and reasonableness before being entered into the comprehensive database. Therefore, the data presented by ITE in the Parking Generation provides a comprehensive average of parking demand for the various land uses throughout the country, and is a recommended resource for the calculation parking demand.

The  $ 85^{th} $ percentile peak parking demand rates from ITE Parking Generation,  $ 5^{th} $ Edition were used in this analysis. An 85% percentile means that of 85% of similar sites survived for the calculation had peak parking demands at or below this rate. This provides a conservative evaluation when calculating the projected parking. The results of this analysis are summarized in Table 6.

<div>Table 6: ITE Parking Generation Parking Demand Summary</div>

<table><tr><td rowspan="3">Land Use</td><td rowspan="3">ITE Land Use Code</td><td rowspan="3">Building</td><td rowspan="3">Size</td><td colspan="5">ITE Parking Generation 5th Edition</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="2">Independent Variable</td><td colspan="2">Peak Period</td><td colspan="2">Peak Parking Demand (veh)</td></tr><tr><td>Parking 85th% Weekday</td><td>Demand Rates Weekend</td><td>Weekday</td><td>Weekend</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="5">Multi-Family Housing Mid-Rise</td><td rowspan="5">221</td><td>Phase 1</td><td>123</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>107</td><td>95</td></tr><tr><td>Phase 2</td><td>153</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>133</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>Phase 3</td><td>153</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>133</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>Phase 4</td><td>153</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>133</td><td>118</td></tr><tr><td>Total</td><td>582</td><td>Bedrooms</td><td>0.87 space/bedroom</td><td>0.77 space/bedroom</td><td>506</td><td>449</td></tr></table>

The ITE Trip Generation 11 $ ^{th} $ Edition LUC 220 describes Multi-Family (Low-Rise) as between 1-3 levels of residential living. The ITE Parking Generation 5 $ ^{th} $ is based upon LUC 221 from the ITE Trip Generation 10 $ ^{th} $ Edition that includes buildings with 3-10 levels of residence.

The projected parking demand calculated was compared to the proposed parking supply for this site to determine if there is adequate parking to accommodate the proposed operations. The highest daily parking demands for this development are expected to occur on the weekdays. The results of the analysis are summarized in Table 7 and show that the proposed parking supply on site will accommodate the projected parking demand, with a peak occupancy of less than 90% utilization.

The peak parking demand for this site was also compared to the City of South Lyon Ordinance requirements which requires 1.5 parking spaces per each dwelling unit. The results of this analysis are summarized in Table 7 and show the City parking supply requirements for this site have a projected surplus parking spaces and would have the site significantly overparked based on the ordinance requirements.
