## 9 KEY FINDINGS

The key findings of this TIS are as follows:

1. Existing Conditions: All approaches and movements at the study intersections are currently operating acceptably, at LOS D or better during both the AM and PM peak hours.

### 2. Background Growth

- A background growth rate of  $ \underline{0.5} $  $ \underline{%} $ was calculated to project traffic volumes to the buildout year of 2027.

The Charleston Park development (195 single family units) was included as background traffic volumes.

<a id="p81"></a>
*[Page 81 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=81)* (read by OCR; check the original)

3. Background Conditions: All approaches and movements at the study intersections are expected to continue operating acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to the existing conditions analysis, with minor increases in delay.

4.  $ \underline{\text{Future Conditions}} $ All approaches and movements at the study intersections are expected to continue operating acceptably, at LOS D or better during both peak periods, in a manner similar to the background conditions analysis, with the exception of the following:

- 10-Mile Road & Dixboro Road: The northbound Dixboro Road approach is expected to operate at LOS E during the AM peak hour. Review of Sim Traffic shows a 95th percentile queue length of approximately 82-feet (3-4 vehicles). Vehicles were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing.

10-Mile Road & Singh Boulevard / Site Drive #1: The northbound left-turn and southbound left-turn movements are expected to operate at LOS E during the PM peak hour. A review of SimTraffic network simulations shows a 95th percentile queue length of approximately 53-feet (2-3 vehicles). Left-turning vehicles were observed to find adequate gaps within the through traffic along 10-Mile Road, without experiencing significant delays or excessive vehicle queueing.

### 5. $ \underline{\text{Parking Analysis}} $

- The results of the parking show that the proposed residential parking supply will accommodate the projected parking demand, with a peak occupancy of less than 90% utilization.

The peak parking demand for this site was also compared to the City of South Lyon Ordinance requirements and show the City parking supply requirements for this site have a projected surplus parking spaces and would have the site significantly overparked based on the ordinance requirements.

## 10 CONCLUSIONS

The conclusions of this TIS are as follows:

- The existing study roadway network can adequately accommodate the projected site-generated traffic volumes. No mitigation measures are recommended.

Any questions related to this memorandum, study, analysis, and results should be addressed to Fleis & VandenBrink.

## Level of Service Criteria for Stop Sign Controlled Intersections

The level of service criteria are given in Exhibit 20-2. As used here, control delay is defined as the total elapsed time from the time a vehicle stops at the end of the queue until the vehicle departs from the stop line; this time includes the time required for the vehicle to travel from the last-in-queue position to the first-in-queue position, including deceleration of vehicles from free-flow speed to the speed of vehicles in queue.

<div>Exhibit 20-2. Level of Service Criteria for Stop-Controlled Intersections (Motor Vehicles)</div>

<table><tr><td>LEVEL OF SERVICE</td><td>AVERAGE CONTROL DELAY (sec/veh)</td></tr><tr><td>A</td><td>≤ 10</td></tr><tr><td>B</td><td>> 10 and ≤ 15</td></tr><tr><td>C</td><td>> 15 and ≤ 25</td></tr><tr><td>D</td><td>> 25 and ≤ 35</td></tr><tr><td>E</td><td>> 35 and ≤ 50</td></tr><tr><td>F</td><td>> 50</td></tr></table>

Average total delay less than 10 sec/veh is defined as Level of Service (LOS) A. Follow-up times of less than 5 sec have been measured when there is no conflicting traffic for a minor street movement, so control delays of less than 10 sec/veh are appropriate for low flow conditions. A total delay of 50 sec/veh is assumed as the break point between LOS E and F.

f q`sldqcdk x sg`m`mt m`lf mi`kydc lmdq`dbsmmr @lditionally, several driver behavior considerations

combine to make delays at signalized intersections less onerous than at unsignalized intersections. For example, drivers at signalized intersections are able to relax during the red interval, where drivers on the minor approaches to unsignalized intersections must remain attentive to the task of identifying acceptable gaps and vehicle conflicts. Also, there is often much more variability in the amount of delay experienced by individual drivers at unsignalized than signalized intersections. For these reasons, it is considered that the total delay threshold for any given level of service is less for an unsignalized intersection than for a signalized intersection.

LOS F exists when there are insufficient gaps of suitable size to allow a side street demand to cross safely through a major street traffic stream. This level of service is generally evident from extremely long total delays experienced by side street traffic and by queueing on the minor approaches. The method, however, is based on a constant critical gap size - that is, the critical gap remains constant, no matter how long the side street motorist waits. LOS F may also appear in the form of side street vehicles' selecting smaller-than-usual gaps. In such cases, safety may be a problem and some disruption to the major traffic stream may result. It is important to note that LOS F may not always result in long queues but may result in adjustments to normal gap acceptance behavior. The latter is more difficult to observe on the field than queueing, which is more obvious.

Source: Highway Capacity Manual, 6th Edition. Transportation Research Board, National Research Council

## Level of Service for Signalized Intersections

Level of service for signalized intersections is defined in terms of delay, which is a measure of driver discomfort and frustration, fuel consumption, and lost travel time. LOS can be characterized for the entire intersection, each intersection approach, and each lane group. Specifically, level-of-service (LOS) criteria are stated in terms of the average stopped delay per vehicle. The criteria are given in Exhibit 19-8. Delay may be measured in the field or estimated using procedures presented later in this chapter. Delay is a complex measure and is dependent on a number of variables, including the quality of progression, the cycle length, the green ratio, and the v/c ratio for the lane group in question.

LOS A describes operations with a control delay of 10 s/veh or less. This level is typically assigned when the volume-to-capacity ratio is low and either progression is extremely favorable or the cycle length is very short. If LOS A is the result of favorable progression, most vehicles arrive during a green indication and travel through the intersection without stopping.

LOS B describes operations with control delay between 10 and 20 s/veh. This level is typically assigned when the volume-to-capacity ratio is low and either progression is highly favorable or the cycle length is short. More vehicles stop than with LOS A.

<div>Exhibit 19.8. Level-of-Service Criteria for Signalized Intersections (Motorized Vehicles)</div>

<table><tr><td>LEVEL OF SERVICE</td><td>STOPPED DELAY PER VEHICLE (SEC)</td></tr><tr><td>A</td><td>≤10.0</td></tr><tr><td>B</td><td>> 10.0 and ≤20.0</td></tr><tr><td>C</td><td>> 20.0 and ≤ 35.0</td></tr><tr><td>D</td><td>> 35.0 and ≤ 55.0</td></tr><tr><td>E</td><td>> 55.0 and ≤ 80.0</td></tr><tr><td>F</td><td>>80.0</td></tr></table>

1. If the v/c ratio for a lane group exceeds 1.0, a LOS F is assigned to the individual lane group. LOS for approach-based and intersection-wide assessments are determined solely by the control delay.

LOS C describes operations with control delay between 20 and 35 s/veh. This level is typically assigned when progression is favorable or the cycle length is moderate. Individual cycle failures (i.e. one or more queued vehicles are not able to depart as a result of insufficient capacity during the cycle) may begin to appear at this level. The number of vehicle stopping is significant, although many vehicles still pass through the intersection without stopping.

LOS D describes operations with control delay between 35 and 55 s/veh. This level is typically assigned when when the volume-to-capacity ratio is high and either progression is ineffective or the cycle length is long. Many vehicles stop and individual cycle failures are noticeable.

LOS E describes operations with control delay between 55 and 80 s/veh. This level is typically assigned when the volume-to-capacity ratio is high, progression is unfavorable, and the cycle length is long. Individual cycle failures are frequent.

LOS F describes operations with control delay exceeding 80 s/veh or a volume-to-capacity ratio greater than 1.0. This level, considered to be unacceptable to most drivers, often occurs with over-saturation, that is, when arrival flow rates exceed the capacity of the intersection. This level is typically assigned when the volume-to-capacity ratio is high, progression is very poor, and the cycle length is long. Most cycles fail to clear the queue.

Source: Highway Capacity Manual, 6th Edition. Transportation Research Board, National Research Council

# CIB COMMUNITY IMAGE BUILDERS PLANNING | MARKETING | ECONOMIC DEVELOPMENT

Jüly 17, 2024

City of South Lyon

335 S. Warren Street

South Lyon, Michigan 48178

Attention: Paul Zelenak, City Manager

Subject: Westlake Conditional Rezoning – Revised Pedestrian Pathway

Dear Mr. Zelenak:

This memo is intended to provide information on the recently proposed changes to the Westlake development. Since approval of the first reading and preliminary site plan, the applicant has proposed modifications to the pedestrian pathways through the development for the purpose of providing safer circulation and a clearer designated pedestrian pathway that runs along the wetlands rather than through the middle of the development. For the new pathway to maintain the 25-foot wetland setback, some shifts in building orientation, the dog park area, parking orientation, and other site elements are required. This memo summarizes the request, the proposed shifts of site element, and offers a side-by-side comparison of the previous plan with the proposed plan.

## BACKGROUND

At its May 13, 2024 meeting, the City Council reviewed a revised conditional rezoning request for the Westlake project, including a modified design of the entrance drive off of Ten Mile using an arched bridge design and other modifications to reduce impacts to the wetlands. The applicant also eliminated the previously proposed boardwalk along Ten Mile that would have impacted the wetlands. The first reading of the revised rezoning request was approved at that meeting.

With the modified entrance design, pedestrian circulation through the site was still maintained, with a sidewalk/path routing through the development. In addition, the updated design included the pedestrian path on the west side of the Ten Mile entrance, meaning pedestrians coming from the east must cross the entrance drive to reach the sidewalk (see graphic below). The applicant has noted that the purpose of this was to further minimize impacts to the wetlands, as there is a smaller wetland area on the west side. This was discussed at the May 13 Council meeting, where it was noted that the sidewalk may be safer for pedestrians if located on the east side of the road so they do not have to cross the entrance drive.

<a id="p152"></a>
*[Page 152 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=152)* (read by OCR; check the original)

<div>Page 2</div>

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

<div>Proposed entrance drive, as presented on May 13, 2024</div>

As noted, the City Council approved the first reading of the conditional rezoning request at the May 13, 2024 meeting.

## CURRENT REQUEST

Since the May 13 City Council meeting, the applicant has approached the City with an updated plan that includes a shift in the pedestrian pathway throughout the development. Changes include moving the pedestrian entrance to the east side of the entrance drive, and moving the pedestrian pathway within the site to go along the wetlands rather than though the middle of the development.

As noted, the previous plan had the pedestrian path winding through the development, which involved sidewalks along parking areas and residential buildings, as well as would require pedestrians to cross parking areas at some points. The updated proposed plan still includes internal sidewalks, but also proposes a pedestrian pathway along the edge of the wetlands on the east side of the development and along Elk Lake Court to Dixboro Road. The applicant has noted that this provides a more scenic route, as well as a clearer designated pedestrian pathway to get between Ten Mile and Dixboro.

The applicant is proposing to maintain the 25-ft. wetland setback and is proposing the pathway outside of the setback area.

To provide the pathway here and maintain the 25-ft. wetland setback, some items along the eastern edge of the development area have shifted. These are listed below:

<a id="p153"></a>
*[Page 153 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=153)* (read by OCR; check the original)

The orientation of Buildings 14, 15, and 16 have shifted slightly

- The dog park has shifted farther back from the wetland and is now completely outside of the 25-ft. setback area

- Slight shift in layout of parking area, elimination of four parking spaces from residential area including two garage spaces and eight industrial spaces

• Shift in garages & dumpster slightly west

• Movement of phase line (Phase 3) slightly (does not impact development)

Along with the new pathway location, the applicant has shifted the pedestrian entrance to the east side of the drive, to better connect into the pathway and eliminate the need for pedestrians to have to cross the entrance drive.

Below are copies of the previous plan and newly proposed plan for your reference and to allow for comparison:

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Plan proposed at May 13, 2024 Council Meeting. Please note that this plan refers to the enlarged revised crossing detail, which is included earlier in this memo.

<a id="p154"></a>
*[Page 154 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=154)* (read by OCR; check the original)

72

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Proposed revised plan (July 16, 2024).

## LEGAL DESCRIPTION

The proposed changes to the plan require modified legal descriptions of the district boundaries tied to the conditional rezoning. The applicant has provided updated legal descriptions, as well as an updated draft of the conditional rezoning agreement reflecting the modified legal descriptions. The City's Engineer has reviewed the updated legal descriptions and provided a review letter of the change. The required shift in the district boundaries means the multiple-family residential portion will be 0.06 acres larger than the original rezoning request, and the industrial will be 0.06 acres smaller than originally proposed. This is a 0.2% increase in the residential area, and is considered a minimal change.

Sincerely,

CIB Planning

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Hannah Smith

Planner

CIB PLANNING

*[Page 156 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=156)* (read by OCR; check the original)

City of South Lyon

335 South Warren

South Lyon, MI 48178

Attn: Ms. Hannah Smith, City Planner

Re: Preliminary Site Plan Review 3

Westlake Planned Unit Development

Dear Ms. McIntyre:

We have reviewed the revised preliminary site plan for the proposed Westlake Planned Unit Development as prepared by Seiber Keast Lehner, Inc (dated June 28, 2024). The revisions to the site layout are minimal and we do not have any objections currently. The utility comments from our February 7, 2024 letter will still need to be addressed in future plan reviews. The comments include:

## Water Supply

1. The water main size, length and type shall be shown on the plans and in profile view for all water supply improvements. All water mains will be required to be looped throughout the site and connect to existing water mains on adjacent properties.

## Sanitary Sewer

1. The sanitary sewer for this site will need to be shown on the plans and tied into the existing four (4) inch force main that leaves the site at the southeast corner. This will require a pump station designed and constructed to City Engineering Design Standards. Connection to the existing sanitary sewer of phase 1 will not be allowed and all sewage should be directed to the new sanitary sewer pump station.

Based on the above, we have no objection to the preliminary site plan approval at this time. The Applicant should address all comments contained herein upon Final Site Plan Submittal. This office is available to discuss the particulars of this review with the Applicant or his engineer, at their request. If you have any questions or require any additional information, please contact the undersigned.

Very truly yours,

HUBBELL, ROTH & CLARK, INC.

Michael P. Darge

# AGENDA NOTE New Business: Item #1

MEETING DATE: July 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Jeff Archey, DPW Superintendent

AGENDA TOPIC: Award Sidewalk Replacement Project to Luigi Ferdinandi and Son Cement Company.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The Sidewalk Replacement Project is being set up to replace any sidewalk that has been heaved or misaligned due to the root system of our city trees. Luigi Ferdinandi and Son Cement Company was awarded the contract last year and did a wonderful job. The contractor will be directed in which areas to address. The plan is to start in a subdivision and complete that area and move to the next. The contractor will be removing and replacing 300 flags of misaligned sidewalks that are creating trip hazards. They will also be removing and disposing of the tree roots that are causing the problem. The contractor is also performing all restoration. This is a job we did budget for this year and it will be coming out of GL 101-441.974.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS:

Quotations from Luigi Ferdinandi and Son, Concrete Construction, Inc. and Hartwell Cement Company

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Award/not award the Sidewalk Replacement Project to Luigi Ferdinandi and Son Cement Company for $89,925.00 from GL account 101-441.974.

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by ___, supported by ___, to award the Sidewalk Replacement Project to Luigi Ferdinandi and Son Cement Company for the cost of $89,925.00 from GL 101-441.974

# AGENDA NOTE New Business Item #:2

MEETING DATE: July 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Jeff Archey, DPW Superintendent

AGENDA TOPIC: Purchase of a 2024 Model Year Freightliner DPW Salt/Plow Dump Truck Chassis Model 108SD Plus.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The DPW would like to replace the 2006 Sterling Large Dump Truck (T-8) due to age and availability of parts for the cab. These trucks are used not only for plowing and salting the city streets, but for hauling of leaf vacuums, dirt, leaf hauling, sweeping debris hauled to the dump and many more uses. We use the MITN bidding system in which the bidding is already completed through the Rochester Hills Cooperative. Wolverine Freightliner received the bid for the cab/chassis and we are using the MIDEAL bidding system for the snow plow equipment, lights and box.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: This quote contains two (2) parts; the cab and chassis. The bid from Wolverine Freightliner includes all the specifications the city needs for an efficient plow truck that is used throughout the year for the projected price of $103,149.00. The price is subject to change and to cover this an additional $6,000 has been added to the projected price.

We need to outfit the truck with snow plow equipment, lights and a box. This will be done through Truck & Trailer Specialties, Inc. for the price of $125,660.00.

In FY 2024-2025 the department budgeted $250,000 for a salt/plow truck in the budget for line item 642-979-500. The total projected cost of this vehicle now is $228,809.00 (not to exceed $234,809.00).

## POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION:

To approve or not approve the purchase of a 2024 Model Year Freightliner DPW Salt/Plow Truck Chassis Model 108SD Plus from Wolverine Freightliner for the projected price of 228,809.00 with the additional 6,000.00 (not to exceed 234,809.00). The following is a 2024 Model Year,  $ \underline{\text{Speculative}} $ Freightliner chassis pricing information per the request. Pricing and conditions are per the proposed Rochester Hills Co-op RFP-RH-20-023 agreement. Actual price is subject to change once we receive formal pricing for 2024 Model Year. This pricing is for budget and forecast purposes.

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by ___, supported by ___, to approve the purchase of a 2024 Model Year Freightliner DPW Salt/Plow Truck Chassis Model 108SD Plus from Wolverine for the amount of $228,809.00.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business: 3

MEETING DATE: July 22, 2024

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Ronald Beason, Water/Sewer Department Superintendent

AGENDA TOPIC: Purchase of new F-250 4x4 Crew Cab Pickup

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: South Lyon Water/Wastewater Department is requesting the purchase of a new F-250 4x4 Crew Cab Pickup. This truck will be an addition to our aging fleet. Observing the operational demands with a full crew, it has become evident that an additional vehicle is necessary. This is a scheduled purchase for this year's budget under line item 642-979-300.

After reviewing three quotes, we recommend the purchase of an F-250 4x4 Crew Cab Pickup from Varsity Ford Ann Arbor in the amount of $52,651.00.

SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Attachment 1: Quote from Varsity Ford Ann Arbor  

Attachment 2: Quote from Hines Park Ford  

Attachment 3: Quote from Brighton Ford

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve/Not approve purchase of a new F-250 4x4 Crew Cab Pickup for $52,651.00 from Varsity Ford Ann Arbor under line 642-979-300.

SUGGESTED MOTION:

Motion by _____, supported by _____ to Approve/Not Approve the purchase of an F-250 4x4 Crew Cab Pick-up from Varsity Ford Ann Arbor for $52,651.00 under line item 642-979-300.

R·R·R·A·S·O·C

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Changes to Your Waste Hauler

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Starting July 1, 2024, trash, recycling, and yard waste collection in Farmington Hills, Village of Milford, Milford Township, Novi, South Lyon, Southfield, Walled Lake, and Wixom is now handled by Priority Waste, which has acquired residential contracts with Green for Life (GFL) for these services.

The most notable changes for residents will be the eventual change in the branding of the collection trucks. There may also be route adjustments made over the first few months and this may result in collection occurring at different times than residents are used to seeing (could be earlier or later). Despite these adjustments, there will be no changes to your collection guidelines (accepted items, disposal instructions, etc.).

As a reminder, trash, recycling, and yard waste should be at the curb for collection by 7:00 a.m. on your designated collection day. To find your community's trash, recycling, or yard waste pickup day, visit our FAQ page.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Celebrate Plastic Free July!

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

Plastic Free July is a global movement followed by millions of people to help prevent and reduce plastic pollution. Reducing your single-use plastic waste usage paves the way for cleaner streets, communities, waterways, and even air! If you're just getting started, try refusing single-use plastics and opt for reusable cups, dinnerware, silverware, takeout boxes, and straws instead. For additional tips, check below!

<a id="p174"></a>
*[Page 174 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=174)* (read by OCR; check the original)

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Coffee Cups

The best reusable cup is the one you already have. Grabbing a cup from home is a simple solution to disposable coffee cups. Popular choices include travel cups made of glass, ceramic, and insulated stainless steel with sealable lids. Many cafes will fill your reusable cup for you.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Shopping Bags

Many people use reusable bags as an alternative to plastic bags, but they are only reusable if you remember them! Return your reusable bags to their usual spot straight after unpacking your groceries so that they are always available, even when making an unexpected grocery trip.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Produce Bags

## Water Bottles

Remember to take a reusable water bottle when on the go! It doesn't need to be anything fancy - even a glass jar or bottle will do! If purchasing a reusable

Buying plastic-wrapped or packaged fruits and vegetables creates unnecessary plastic waste. Consider looking out for loose fruit and vegetables in your local grocery store or farmers market. Also, choose lightweight, reusable produce bags instead of the single-use plastic bags that are usually provided.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Plastic Straws

Single-use straws are used for a few minutes then discarded, where they'll remain in the environment indefinitely. Bring your own sustainable alternatives when out and about. There are stainless steel, bamboo, and glass options - plus, foldable options that are easy to transport in your purse or pocket!

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

## Baked Goods

Choose to refuse pre-packed baked goods. By using your own reusable bread bag or containers when buying unpackaged baked goods, you can easily

<a id="p175"></a>
*[Page 175 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/7-22-24%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202407181618280#page=175)* (read by OCR; check the original)

water bottle, consider its lifespan. Stainless steel is a popular option as it is a durable material. Many public venues now have water bottle refill stations.

avoid single-use plastic packaging. If you forget to bring your own, you can also ask for a paper bag that can be recycled later.

Want to learn more about Plastic Free July and how you can make a difference at a community or government level? Visit the Plastic Free July website!

## 2024 HHW Collection Event Dates

During the following HHW Collection Events, all residents of RRRASOC member communities are welcome to bring household hazardous waste for an opportunity to safely discard their hazardous items and e-waste. Simple Recycling and paper shredding services will also be available $ ^{*} $.

October 5, 2024

Novi Dept. of Public Works

26300 Lee BeGole Dr., Novi

9:00am - 2:00pm

October 26, 2024

RRRASOC MRF - Truck Entrance

20875 Mapleridge Ave., Southfield

9:00am - 2:00pm

 $ ^{*} $The T-shirt icon indicates an HHW Collection Event during which clothing, shoes, accessories, and small household items will be accepted by Simple Recycling.

For more information about HHW events and how to prepare your items, please visit our HHW page.

## HHW Drop-Off at ERG Environmental Services

If you have HHW that you'd like to properly dispose of between collection events, you're in luck! Drop off your HHW without a fee at ERG Environmental Services during the following hours:

Monday - Friday: 9:00 AM - 4:00 PM

HHW pickup from your home is also available for a fee of $135 per stop plus $0.75 per pound of material collected. This fee is paid directly by the resident to ERG Environmental Services.

To confirm drop-off times and/or to schedule a pickup, please contact:

ERG Environmental Services

13040 Merriman Road, Livonia

(734) 437-9650

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

For more info, visit the ERG HHW Drop-Off page or our HHW page.
